# Rust → Lean HTTP parity audit

Date: 2026-11-12. Baseline: `4199fc06e^` = `b22ed3181df64cf9681e887381ede7ed15fe96aa` (Rust, read with `git show`); target: `5383bf27b8f8e664b654339cf88e14c90dba665c` (HEAD). This is a source audit, not a deployment certification.

**Executive result:** 545 explicit historical method/path pairs inventoried: **E 0 / D 13 / S 22 / M 227 / U 283**. Seventeen new concrete findings (HTTP-01…HTTP-17) have source evidence, client impact/consumer gaps, confidence and a one-PR repair outline. The highest-impact confirmed regressions are password-rotation fallback, unusable uploads, false backup/restore and software-update success, destructive chain/project partial updates, and global/fabricated reliability data. Route census is complete; behavioral parity verification is not. No E is inferred from route existence. Existing managed-provider/lifecycle/recovery findings retain their owners.

## Scope, method and limits

- Read root `AGENTS.md`, `docs/plans/core-recovery-2026-11.md` and `docs/plans/managed-providers-leanci-colab.md`. No source, test, service, credentials or configuration changes; no builds, commits or live writes. Only this document is an audit artifact.
- Enumerate method/path pairs from the historical `src/api/routes.rs`, recursively expanding its `.nest` and `.merge` routers, including chained methods. Exclude test-only routers. Resolve handler definitions and Rust return types separately from registration. Include explicit `ANY`; Axum's implicit HEAD-for-GET behavior is a separate transport concern, not an invented explicit registration.
- Trace current dispatch in `Control.lean` (including ordering), `R7Core.lean` (parser/body limits, not the main router), and delegated `SecretAndApproval`, `RemoteFleetAndRelay`, `ProjectFilesAndController`, `CloudHttpAndWorker`, `ResourcesAndSystem`, `ProviderUsageAndLogin` and proxy builders. Registration/dispatch candidates alone never establish equivalence.
- Independently compare expected behavior (old serializers, caller requests and caller consumption) with Lean responses and effects. A proof/test of the same implementation formula would not establish this contract. At this audit’s start, requested specialist methodologies were not available and the memory credential file was absent. No credential recovery was attempted here. The coordinator subsequently reported reading restored Library methodologies and Hindsight context independently; that companion review is not represented as evidence this reader inspected.
- Client sweep includes all source files under `orb/src`, `ios_dashboard`, `android_dashboard` and JSON under `shared/orb-spec`: declared types, dynamic/dictionary readers, request paths, mutation payloads, and response projections. The appendices distinguish a mechanical type/path census from a manually traced contract. A TypeScript generic is not runtime validation; Swift/Kotlin dictionary fallbacks can conceal missing fields.
- The coordinator reports a public dev GET `/api/health` returning 200 (`status:ok`, `version:0.7.10`, `dev_mode:false`, `auth_required:true`, `auth_mode:single_tenant`, `max_iterations:50`). Protected and nonexistent paths both returned 401 `{"error":"unauthorized"}`. Those observations do **not** establish that protected routes exist or are missing. No authenticated replay, network effects, stream longevity, or deployed revision was verified here.

### Register legend

**E** = verified equivalent shape/status/default/authorization/effect for the stated contract; **D** = concrete difference; **S** = implemented success-shaped stub/no-op or fabricated observation; **M** = no reachable implementation found for the stated method/path; **U** = not yet verified. No blanket E is assigned by this audit. A U is an open verification obligation, not a pass. Named Rust return types in the register specify the expected success schema, with fields expanded in the schema appendix where resolvable. `Result<…, …>` also points to the error contract; it is not a promise that every error branch was compared. `opaque` explicitly means the success body remains unverified. Lean candidate lines on U rows are navigation aids, not proof that that method reaches that branch. `Control.lean:5730` is the authenticated final 404; absence claims refer to the dispatcher and its delegated routers, not live unauthenticated probes.

Paths below are repository-relative for portability; all Rust references mean the pinned historical tree, all Lean/client references mean HEAD. Mount-site references retain the route's connection to `src/api/routes.rs`.

## New concrete findings

Severity is relative to user impact; one-PR fixes below are bounded suggestions, not changes made in this audit. “Code-confirmed” certifies the cited source difference, not a successful exploit or live reproduction.

### HTTP-01 — P1: password rotation leaves static old credentials as an alternate login

**Code-confirmed.** Rust `src/api/auth.rs:467–480` gives the stored password verdict precedence: `Some(false)` rejects without falling back to the environment. Lean `Control.lean:1933–1942` tries the persisted user's PBKDF2 hash, then accepts `verifyLoginCredentials` or the settings password even after failure; `Control.lean:400–410` checks the unchanged `dashboardPassword` / configured users. For an enabled persisted account whose remaining static credential matches the fallback verifier, changing its persisted password does not remove that alternate credential. In the bootstrap case, `username:"admin"` (or omitted username) plus the old configured dashboard password can therefore still mint a fresh token while that configuration remains set. Existing token epoch revocation does not close this alternate issuance path. This is distinct from accepting an old already-issued token.

Client evidence: desktop `orb/src/accountAdmin.ts:144–149`, iOS `OrbGitHubSignIn.swift:347–349`, Android `OrbAccountAdmin.kt:202–206` present this as password rotation and send `current_password` / `new_password`.

**One PR:** establish one authoritative verifier per persisted account; only migrate static credentials when no persisted hash exists, never after a negative stored verdict. Preserve explicit dev-mode behavior separately. Add old-password rejection/new-password acceptance cases for named and omitted bootstrap username and configured-user migration. Confirm compatibility with settings-hash migration before removing it.

### HTTP-02 — P1: attachment upload stores the JSON envelope and returns no usable file path

**Code-confirmed.** Rust `src/api/uploads.rs:45–107` parses `{node_id,…Upload}`, forwards to the requested machine or decodes/stores the file, returning `src/uploads.rs:114–119` `{name,path,size,sha256}`. Lean `Control.lean:2594–2610` writes the entire request body verbatim under a random ID, ignores `node_id`, name and `data_base64`, then returns 201 `{id,size,owner_user_id}`. It does not supply the caller's expected path. This silently misplaces remote attachments and stores JSON instead of the file bytes.

Clients: `orb/src/uploads.ts:431–444` sends JSON and explicitly throws when `receipt.path` is absent; `orb/src/imageAttachments.ts:43` expects `{path}`. iOS `OrbConversation+Sync.swift:620` and `OrbSideQuestion.swift:1363` send the same JSON. Android `OrbAttachmentsAndImages.kt:110–137` instead tries raw bytes and accepts `id` as a path fallback: it may appear successful while inserting an opaque UUID that is not the stored filesystem path. That Android fallback is a pre-existing cross-client protocol divergence, not evidence that Lean matches Rust.

**One PR:** restore the JSON upload/receipt contract, decoding before storage and dispatching `node_id` via the shared transfer path; reject unsupported content types rather than accepting envelopes as files. Align the Android raw/multipart path with the common protocol and add cross-client receipt fixtures.

### HTTP-03 — P1: resumable upload session protocol is absent

**Code-confirmed.** Rust registrations `src/api/routes.rs:950–962` expose POST session init, PUT indexed chunk, POST complete and DELETE cancel, backed by `src/api/uploads.rs` session handlers and `src/uploads.rs:122–142` (`SessionInit`/`SessionStatus`). No `/api/uploads/sessions` dispatch exists in current `Control.lean` or delegated routers; authenticated requests fall through at `Control.lean:5730`. The raw POST `/api/uploads` does not match these paths.

Client evidence: `orb/src/uploads.ts:306–315` constructs all session paths and `:636` cancels pending sessions; large direct-machine uploads choose this flow at `:423–427`. Mobile attachment paths do not implement this desktop session contract (gap, not parity).

**One PR:** port the init/chunk/complete/cancel state machine and receipt validation with bounded chunks, idempotent finalize and remote node forwarding. Return an explicit unsupported error until implemented, not fallback success from another upload path.

### HTTP-04 — P1: backup and restore acknowledge success without doing either operation

**Code-confirmed.** Rust `src/api/settings.rs:469–554` returns a ZIP archive with download headers; restore `:593–639` requires multipart backup/file data, validates it and reports restored files (`RestoreBackupResponse`, `:586`). Lean `Control.lean:2612–2616` checks admin then returns 200 `{"ok":true}` for either path, regardless of method/body. A downloaded “backup” is JSON, and even an invalid/empty restore is reported successful.

Clients: desktop `orb/src/accountAdmin.ts:327–401` generates multipart restore data and downloads backup bytes; iOS `OrbDebugInfo.swift:398,421` and Android `OrbDebugInfo.kt:667,857` expose the same export/import operation.

**One PR:** implement archive export/import, content validation and precise restored-file receipt behind existing approval policy; until then fail explicitly (501/503), never success. Verify invalid archives cannot mutate files and that an exported archive round-trips in a temporary store.

### HTTP-05 — P1: software update/cancel are fabricated jobs and use the wrong schema

**Code-confirmed.** Rust `src/api/agent_software.rs:336–359` consumes a singular `{component,version,path}` and queues a real update job; cancel `:361–378` consumes `{id}`, actually cancels and returns `{ok:true}`, not a job DTO. Lean `ResourcesAndSystem.lean:1431–1462` reads `components` (plural), creates a fresh in-memory response already marked `status:"succeeded"`, never installs/queues anything, and cancel ignores the ID and returns a made-up `id:"cancelled"` job. The update response is 202 `{job:…}` rather than the Rust job object; uses `status`/`components` rather than the existing `state`/`component` contract.

Clients: desktop `orb/src/softwareInventory.ts:9–10,92,107` defines `SoftwareJob` (`component,version,path,state,error,created_at,updated_at`) and sends singular update/cancel payloads; iOS `OrbAgentSoftware.swift:30–54,346,358` and Android `OrbAgentSoftware.kt:59–80,373,506,531` mirror the contract. A 2xx acknowledgement is not installation evidence.

**One PR:** connect a persistent real update/cancel queue to the existing scanner and publish the existing job schema in inventory. If the install path cannot ship in that PR, return unsupported instead of fabricating successful work.

### HTTP-06 — P1: chain list/create/update responses no longer match any routing client; partial update is destructive

**Code-confirmed.** Rust `src/api/model_routing.rs:46–60,84–101,187–204` returns `{id,name,entries:[{provider_id,model_id}],is_default,strip_thinking,created_at,updated_at}` and applies optional patch fields to an existing chain. Lean `Control.lean:4496–4514` lists `{id,name,models_json,updated_at}`; create/update `:4515–4534` returns only `{id,name}`. Its `INSERT OR REPLACE` defaults omitted models/entries to `[]` and name to ID. A client PUT `{is_default:true}` therefore replaces the chain's entries with `[]` and never sets the default; `strip_thinking` is also ignored.

Clients: `orb/src/routingApi.ts:8–21,127,138–149`; iOS `OrbAdminSettings.swift:49,221,260`; Android `OrbAdminSettings.kt:262,458,772–774`. The desktop default action sends exactly the dangerous partial body.

**One PR:** use the historical response DTO and merge optional updates into the existing persisted chain, preserving entries/name unless explicitly supplied; honor default/thinking flags and validation. Cover create→list→set-default without entry loss in a single independent contract fixture.

### HTTP-07 — P1: chain delete/get and other unmatched routing paths return false 200 success

**Code-confirmed.** Rust get/delete `src/api/model_routing.rs:92–101,250–266` fetches/removes a specific chain, rejects built-ins and distinguishes missing/conflict. For ordinary GET/DELETE `/api/model-routing/chains/:id`, Lean delegated provider dispatch has no chain CRUD branch; `Control.lean:4535–4536` returns 200 `{"ok":true,"router":"/api/model-routing"}` with no read/delete. Other unmatched paths in the same prefix also get this catch-all. The DELETE no-op applies to authorized admin/legacy callers; a non-admin mutation can instead receive 403 at `Control.lean:4465–4467`. The explicit resolve handler at `Control.lean:4468–4480` is implemented and is not part of this absence/no-op claim. This is not equivalent to an empty chain.

Clients: desktop `orb/src/routingApi.ts:150–153` calls delete/resolve; iOS routing path `OrbAdminSettings.swift:49` and Android delete `OrbAdminSettings.kt:801` treat successful mutation as acceptance.

**One PR:** implement exact get/delete handlers with persistence and built-in protection; return 404/405 for unmatched path/method instead of generic success. Keep separate from HTTP-06's DTO/update repair if necessary.

### HTTP-08 — P1: routing health uses incompatible field names and fabricated healthy quota

**Code-confirmed.** Rust model health routes `src/api/model_routing.rs:33–35` return the account-health contract consumed by clients. Lean `ProviderUsageAndLogin.lean:1067–1100` emits `remaining_quota_pct`, `cooldown_secs_remaining`, `sample_count`, `ewma_ttft_ms` etc., not `is_healthy`, `cooldown_remaining_secs`, `total_requests`, `total_successes`, `total_rate_limits`, `total_errors` or `rate_limit_snapshot`. It defaults missing quota to 100 and sample counts to zero. This hides unknown/unobserved health behind optimistic numbers.

Clients: desktop `orb/src/routingApi.ts:52–73,128`; iOS `OrbAdminSettings.swift:155,222` and Android `OrbAdminSettings.kt:263,576` consume/clear account-health rows.

**One PR:** adapt the live breaker/counter data to the established DTO with explicit unknown/null observations; never synthesize full quota when unavailable. Include empty/unknown, cooldown and auth-failure cases.

### HTTP-09 — P1: reliability endpoint reads unscoped aggregate counts/latest failures and reports an invented success rate

**Code-confirmed.** Rust `src/api/routes.rs:2489–2513` uses the authenticated user's control/mission store and `proxy_reliability::summarize`/series. Lean `ProviderUsageAndLogin.lean:998–1048` reads aggregate counts and the latest 20 failures from shared `proxy_outcomes`, not the entire outcome set. Aggregate selection has only timestamp filtering and no caller/mission owner constraint (caller arguments are available but unused here); the latest-failures query is not even filtered by the requested window. Cross-user disclosure is conditional on that shared table containing other users’ data. The independent review found no current Lean `proxy_outcomes` producer, so this audit does not establish a present populated-data disclosure. The unscoped queries, literal rate, window mismatch and incompatible DTO are source-confirmed. For any nonzero request count, `success_rate` is literally `0.99`, even if every request failed. The payload also replaces required reliability `groups`/series with `by_chain`/`by_provider` empty arrays.

Clients: `orb/src/proxyHealthApi.ts` defines the reliability response and `orb/src/ReliabilityView.tsx:20,33` displays its success rate; desktop routing/usage UIs are the traced consumers. No equivalent mobile reliability DTO was found in the client sweep; absence of a consumer does not relax the HTTP confidentiality requirement.

**One PR:** scope outcome selection by authorized caller/mission, apply the window to all selected data, compute real statistics and serialize the existing reliability DTO. Test a two-user dataset and an all-failure window; do not derive expected rates from the current `0.99` implementation.

### HTTP-10 — P2: timing endpoint loses multi-user denial and returns a synthetic all-zero observation

**Code-confirmed.** Rust `src/api/routes.rs:2430–2448` returns 403 in multi-user mode because timing is process-wide, clamps minutes to 1…1440 and returns the actual timing summary. Lean `ProviderUsageAndLogin.lean:1050–1064` always returns 200, permits up to 10080 minutes, and fabricates zero samples/quantiles. Current zero payload does not itself reveal another user's traffic; no current cross-user timing disclosure is established. The confirmed findings are the missing multi-user boundary, changed clamp and synthetic observation. The removed boundary would become a confidentiality bug if real process-wide timings were later wired in unchanged.

Client evidence: no current `/api/ai/proxy/timing` caller or dedicated timing DTO was found across desktop, iOS, Android or shared fixtures. `orb/src/proxyHealthApi.ts` is the distinct reliability endpoint, not a timing client. Thus this is an HTTP/default/security-boundary regression, not a claimed current mobile rendering failure. **One PR:** restore mode-based denial first, then expose actual observations or an explicit unavailable status; preserve the 24-hour clamp unless deliberately versioned.

### HTTP-11 — P1: downloaded user/agent content loses browser sandbox headers

**Code-confirmed header regression; suspected exploitability.** Rust `src/api/fs.rs:170–180,1261–1298` sets `X-Content-Type-Options:nosniff`, sandbox CSP and, for mission media, attachment disposition; ordinary download also calls the same hardener. Lean `ResourcesAndSystem.lean:1311–1317` returns `(200,inferMimeType,bytes)`. Delegated response construction at `Control.lean:5722–5728` and encoding `:1134–1157` add no security headers, and no global CSP/nosniff hardener exists in this path. Similar raw file responses need the same review. An SVG served from the authenticated API origin must not become active same-origin content.

Clients: desktop file/media surfaces consume `/api/fs/download`; Android `OrbAttachmentsAndImages.kt:472–475,799–801` builds direct download/image URLs; iOS generic file/client transport also consumes downloaded bytes. Whether a particular WebView/browser navigation executes SVG was **not** tested; only the missing defense is code-confirmed.

**One PR:** add one shared download response constructor carrying MIME/disposition/CSP/nosniff (including delegated raw-byte responses), preserving media mission authorization. Test headers for SVG, HTML-like unknown extensions, PDF and media without rendering untrusted content.

### HTTP-12 — P1: chunk upload accepts multipart as bytes and finalize silently omits missing chunks

**Code-confirmed.** Rust `src/api/fs.rs:1457–1506` parses multipart and checks index/count/chunk-size bounds; finalize `:1585–1624` errors when a chunk is missing and enforces assembled size. Lean `ResourcesAndSystem.lean:1369–1397` stores raw body bytes as `chunk_N`, defaults missing/invalid index to zero, and appends only chunks that can be read. Missing chunks are skipped, followed by 200 and deletion of staging data. Even correctly transmitted multipart chunks include envelope bytes in the assembled file. This is silent file corruption, independent of HTTP-02's newer JSON upload route.

Client evidence: legacy filesystem upload consumers coexist with the newer uploader; desktop `orb/src/uploads.ts` primarily uses HTTP-02/03, not proof this legacy path is safe. Generic mobile/file-service paths exist, but no current end-to-end chunk caller was manually verified; client reachability for this legacy contract is U.

**One PR:** implement the declared multipart protocol, strict indices/counts/byte budgets and fail-atomic finalize; do not remove staging on failure. Test missing middle chunk, duplicate/out-of-range index, over-budget assembly and exact resulting bytes.

### HTTP-13 — P2: capability discovery has been replaced with unrelated optimistic flags

**Code-confirmed.** Rust `src/api/capabilities.rs:31–66,74–128` returns `{capabilities:{mission_dispatch,github_push,dashboard_github_login}}` with `{available,detail,remedy?}` based on observed workspace/GitHub state. Lean `Control.lean:5685–5686` instead returns constant top-level `mission_moves,project_folders,mcp_gateway,managed_relay:true`. Agents lose both the reason and remedy for a blocked dispatch/push; these are not renamed equivalents.

Client evidence: no dedicated typed desktop/iOS/Android `/api/capabilities` consumer was found; the historical handler explicitly supports authenticated autonomous clients. Current dashboard uses separate auth capabilities; do not conflate `auth/status.capabilities` with this endpoint.

**One PR:** restore the structured observed capability response, deriving each verdict from the same authority used by execution. Keep additional feature flags in a versioned field if needed, not instead of the existing contract.

### HTTP-14 — P2: aggregate stats response is replaced by a global count

**Code-confirmed.** Rust `src/api/routes.rs:2240–2287` scopes tasks/mission counts to the authenticated user and honors `since`; `src/api/types.rs:37–59` requires total/active/completed/failed tasks, cost provenance and success rate. Lean `Control.lean:5688–5692` ignores `since`, counts all missions and returns only `{missions:N}`. This both breaks the old shape and exposes a global count to ordinary authenticated users.

Client evidence: desktop request scheduling still recognizes `/api/stats` (`orb/src/api.ts:238`, `transportScheduler.ts:59`); no current dedicated stats DTO/rendering consumer was verified in the four-tree sweep. API compatibility and tenant isolation still require explicit treatment even if a UI stopped polling.

**One PR:** scope and aggregate into `StatsResponse`, preserving the window and actual/estimated/unknown provenance, or explicitly retire/version the endpoint with callers migrated. Do not label a global count an equivalent stats implementation.

### HTTP-15 — P2: Vibe loses native plan mode despite an otherwise matching backend DTO

**Code-confirmed.** Rust `src/api/backends.rs:25–34` marks `codex`, `claudecode`, `vibe`, `antigravity` as native-plan capable. Lean `Control.lean:4355–4364` hard-codes `vibe.native_plan:false`. This is a useful counterexample to checking only field names: `{id,name,native_plan}` matches structurally but the value contract does not.

Client evidence: `orb/src/api.ts:1266–1268` defines `BackendInfo`; `orb/src/composer/Composer.tsx:502` conditions composer modes on `backend.native_plan`. Android `OrbCore.kt:2672` loads backend rows; mobile-specific plan projection was not fully traced. This finding establishes the Core backend capability regression and the desktop composer impact, not equivalent impact across all mobile clients.

**One PR:** derive native-plan support from one backend capability source rather than a second hard-coded table, and add a backend-capability fixture consumed by all three clients.

### HTTP-16 — P1: legacy secret lock/unlock ignores passphrases and always reports unlocked

**Code-confirmed; independent review narrowed the security claim.** For an existing encrypted secret, historical Rust `src/api/secrets.rs:214–243` validates the unlock passphrase, returns 401 on failure, and lock clears the in-memory passphrase. Historical Rust unlock on an empty store already accepted an arbitrary passphrase, so unconditional passphrase acceptance is not a new regression in that case. Lean `SecretAndApproval.lean:1194–1196` treats initialize, unlock and lock identically: creates/loads a private key, ignores the supplied request/passphrase and returns 200 `{ok:true,unlocked:true}`. The current admin gate still applies. Status `:1185–1187` equates loadable key availability with unlocked state; that availability includes environment sources via `LegacyHydrate.lean:70–87`, not just key-file presence. A lock request explicitly reports the system unlocked. This is distinct from the managed-provider reserved-key policy already owned by the child plan; no secret-reveal bypass or Bitwarden biometric bypass is proven.

Client evidence: no current `/api/secrets/{lock,unlock,initialize}` caller was found in desktop/iOS/Android; current mobile/desktop secrets screens call `/api/bitwarden/*` instead (`orb/src/bitwardenApi.ts:85–121`, iOS `OrbBitwardenSettings.swift:710–742`, Android `OrbBitwardenSettings.kt:419–476`). Do not misattribute this legacy HTTP regression to Bitwarden's client-side biometric lock. No claim is made that all legacy reveal operations remain reachable; most legacy registry routes are missing.

**One PR:** either restore a real lock/passphrase authority and its established success/error schema, or explicitly retire legacy lock/unlock with an unsupported response and migrate remaining external consumers. Never return successful unlock for a lock or invalid passphrase.

### HTTP-17 — P1: changing project color overwrites persisted title and status

**Code-confirmed.** Rust `src/api/projects_overview.rs:813–841` changes only color on an existing project, returning 404 for unknown projects. Lean `ProjectFilesAndController.lean:1217–1224` first calls `upsertProjectRecord` with `title=clientSlug,status="active"`. The upsert at `:239–249` overwrites nonempty title/name/status on conflict. Therefore setting color on an archived/paused persisted project with a human title replaces the persisted title with the slug and resets its database status to active. This proves persisted title/status destruction, not unconditional visible reactivation: project archive/pause actions at `ProjectFilesAndController.lean:1185–1204` also write `board-overrides.json`, and roster logic at `:160–162,220–229` gives those overrides precedence. An existing override may keep the project visibly archived/paused despite database corruption. Other callers of the same upsert with placeholder title/default active status (skills/controller/status actions) need the same preservation check; the proven minimal reproducer is appearance. Independent review confirmed the source defect while narrowing its visible-impact claim.

Clients: desktop `orb/src/projectAppearance.ts:43–61` sends only color; iOS `OrbProjectAppearance.swift:35` and Android `OrbTheme.kt:466` do likewise. None asks to rename or reactivate the project.

**One PR:** separate create defaults from optional update fields in the shared project store, make appearance an existing-record color-only update, and cover title/status/color independence across all clients. Avoid per-route workarounds that leave other partial upserts destructive.

## Independent review status

The coordinator reports a completed independent second-reader review of HTTP-01…HTTP-17: **12 confirmed / 5 narrowed**. The narrowed findings are HTTP-09, HTTP-10, HTTP-11, HTTP-16 and HTTP-17; all seventeen source findings survive with the qualifications incorporated above. Across these seventeen HTTP findings and MCP-01…MCP-17 in the companion audit, the final reported verdict is **34 checked: 24 confirmed / 10 narrowed**. These are finding-review counts, not route classifications or an assertion that this HTTP reader independently audited the MCP findings. No HTTP finding remains pending this review. In particular, HTTP-11 confirms missing headers, not exploitability; HTTP-12’s current legacy-client reachability remains U; HTTP-15’s demonstrated client impact is desktop, not all mobile clients; HTTP-16 proves misleading lock/unlock behavior, not a secret-reveal or biometric bypass; HTTP-17 proves persisted title/status destruction, not unconditional visible reactivation. No new source research or runtime testing was performed to apply these prose refinements.

## Verification priorities for the second reader

1. Recheck HTTP-01 against the persisted-account/bootstrap migration and settings-password write path; the fallback issuance branch is proven, but evaluate all migrated account configurations before assigning an exploit severity beyond P1.
2. Reproduce HTTP-02/03/04/05/06/07 with a temporary isolated store and independent expected responses; no provider spend or live mutation is needed. These are the highest-confidence user-visible failures.
3. Review route ordering, not just all branch bodies: managed reserved GETs are already shadowed; cloud/project helper calls have both early and late call sites. Method guards and authorization must be included in a follow-up E decision.
4. For every U row, inspect both response construction and client projection, plus errors/status/defaults and effects. For streams compare frames, cursor headers and reconnection semantics, not only 200/101. Check Axum implicit HEAD, 405 behavior, gzip and CORS separately.
5. Missing endpoint families in the register (legacy task/run API, OpenCode connections/config, much of workspaces, validation campaigns, mesh mutations, system components) need explicit product retirement decisions or implementations. Do not silently replace them with generic 200 replies.
6. Do not use generated fixture contents or Lean theorem names as independent evidence of HTTP equivalence. `shared/orb-spec` is predominantly kernel/projection data; managed-machine, mission-budget, approvals and telemetry fixtures cover narrow response/projection contracts, not the full REST surface.

## Exhaustive historical method/path register

Extracted **545 unique explicit method/path pairs**: 248 declared directly in `routes.rs`, the remainder recursively mounted. Explicit `ANY` is retained; implicit Axum HEAD behavior still needs transport comparison. Expected shape is the historical handler return signature, not an inference from the URL. Client literals are source candidates, not validated consumers.

### Exact absence/exclusion trace

M is assigned only after checking the complete main dispatcher `Control.lean:1896–5730` plus its helpers, not by failure of a string search or a protected live 401. A static path can also be shadowed by a generic `:id` branch; that is still missing the intended operation. Source access/auth can reject earlier than final 404, particularly `/api/mcp/*`; M does not promise every credential receives 404.

| Excluded surface | Complete relevant dispatch evidence / implemented exceptions |
| --- | --- |
| Public GitHub dashboard OAuth; webhook/Telegram/internal actions; Hermes remote proxy; speed proxy; Spark/remote-build; bunker public routes | Main public chain `Control.lean:1908–2418` contains health/login, WS, `/v1/*`, privilege verify, MCP and managed relay/bootstrap only. Later helpers do not add these prefixes. GitHub integration device flow at `ResourcesAndSystem.lean:1700–1761` is **not** dashboard OAuth login. |
| Legacy task/run API, `/api/memory/search`, OpenCode/Claude config/connections/restart, tools, monitoring memory-health, validation campaigns, desktop sessions, backend detail/quota/agents/config | Main dispatcher plus all delegated dispatchers have no branch. Workspaces root `Control.lean:2552–2579` and shell upgrade `:1957` do not implement workspace CRUD/build/sync/exec/resources/preflight subroutes. Backends root `:4355` does not implement per-ID methods. |
| Legacy MCP server CRUD/refresh; session delete; wait requests | Gateway `Control.lean:2071–2327` implements external sessions, session, renew, capabilities and call; no legacy `/api/mcp` registry methods, session-delete or wait handler. Authentication can mask absence. |
| Assistant gateways, Telegram bridges/bots/actions/workflows, Paloma read APIs; control progress/tracks/fleet-health/cancel/metrics/perf/client-link/ws | Main dispatcher and `ResourcesAndSystem.lean:1763–2136` cover speed/running/client-computers/local-origins/search/digest/stats/client-run/status/transcript/send-now, not these historical operations. `/api/control/sync` is not `/api/control/ws` or `/api/control/client-link`. |
| Board/task-verdict, tree/load/status/origin/mode/pause/purge/clone/parallel/force-slot, old `/btw`, ask threads, import/export/cleanup, standalone automation GET/PATCH/DELETE/executions | Main mission branch list and `dispatchMissionControlAndGitRoutes?` (`Control.lean:1623–1893`) do not implement these exact suffixes. `/board-task`, `/btw/context`, `/btw/agent`, fork, interrupt, stop, and automation `/:id/action` are distinct paths, not aliases inferred by this audit. Static `current`/`integrity` GETs can hit generic mission-ID lookup. |
| Shadow-prices, managed reconcile/raw operations | Managed/control dispatch and `RemoteFleetAndRelay.lean:997–1297` omit these methods. `GET :id/operations`'s empty list is not the historical POST raw-operation route. Reconcile remains owned Recovery §2. |
| Legacy secret encryption/key/registries; Bitwarden per-project secret CRUD/reveal; privilege approve-all; settings llm-roles/library-remote | `SecretAndApproval.lean:915–1399` implements named list/status/config/broker/approval/default-access routes, but not these exact historical methods. Main settings/proxy handlers `Control.lean:5641–5683` add none. Bitwarden `/secrets` collection is not per-secret CRUD/reveal. |
| Durable start/logs; system components/reconcile/Hermes management except status | `RemoteFleetAndRelay.lean:1263–1297` implements list/get and cancel/force state writes, not start or log reads. `ResourcesAndSystem.lean:1556–1607` implements `/system/info`, processes and Hermes status/control/logs; these do not alias historical components/adopt/stop/remote/skills/reconcile routes. Deploy is separately implemented/owned. |
| Project imports, conversation PUT/DELETE, context upload sessions; library historical revision read | `Control.lean:5424–5522` handles direct context manifest/conflicts/history/stream/blob/operations, not upload sessions. `ProjectFilesAndController.lean:853–1353` handles files/controller/crons/project state, but no imports or conversation binding writes. A GET conversation stub is not PUT/DELETE. `ResourcesAndSystem.lean:1497–1554` has empty history but no `history/:rev` read. |
| Provider legacy auth/opencode-auth/OAuth callback/session attachment/Mistral login | `ProviderUsageAndLogin.lean:1148–1479` implements CLIProxy login, types, reorder, for-backend, usage/default/health/reset/recheck/auth-methods, not these other authentication/session endpoints. `Control.lean:4203–4352` adds provider root/ID CRUD/preferences, not those missing suffixes. |

## Client type and projection census

Read **545 TypeScript/TSX/Swift/Kotlin source files** as data. The following register includes every detected named declaration in files with HTTP paths, typed/dictionary JSON parsing, or API/model/projection filenames. UI-only declarations within those files are retained. Whole-tree counts below distinguish central transport files from the wider client model surface. This census is mechanical and is not semantic verification of every type: declarations and matching field names alone cannot establish decoder compatibility. Dynamic `OrbDict`/`OrbJSON`/JVal field readers also define contracts. Only findings above assert manually traced behavior; the rest remain U.

All JSON above was parsed as data. None is a comprehensive HTTP method/status/schema suite. Especially relevant are `managed-machines-response.json`, `mission-budget.json`, `usage-telemetry.json`, `multiuser-rbac.json`, `approvals.json` and `privilege-access.json`; fixture presence cannot certify endpoint authorization, reachability, effects or errors. ABI/C-ABI/WASM projections cover a different layer.

## Completion boundary

The route register is exhaustive for explicit historical registrations reachable from `src/api/routes.rs`, including nested/merged router methods. Behavioral audit is **not** labeled exhaustive parity verification: U rows, opaque serializers, untraced dynamic client consumers and network/stream behavior remain open. No E claim is based on route existence, self-test names or implementation-derived fixtures. New findings are HTTP-01…HTTP-17; managed/recovery issues retain their existing owners.
